ParlaMint-GR_2019-10-31-S1-commons.u314	The President of the Government, Mr. Kyriakos Mitsotakis, is called in Stepma.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-10-31-S1-commons.u315	Thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, a month ago and in the context of the Prime Minister's time, from this step, both in my prology and in my denology, I described our policy towards the successive waves of unscrupulous and outdated waves that our country unfortunately accepts every day. I had spoken about the need to protect our borders. I had spoken about the necessary separation between refugees and economic migrants. I have obviously spoken about the European dimension of the problem, but I have also spoken of the rules under which those who deserve it will be able to remain in our home country will be incorporated into Greek society under human conditions. And today the draft law which will become the law of the state when this debate is completed is completing the coverage of a great gap. Greece finally acquires a clear, structured and operational asylum system. I would like to congratulate the Minister and the Deputy Minister for this legislative initiative, for the procedure that has been followed, for the fact that many of the comments made in public consultation have been incorporated in the text of the final draft law, which was tabled for the vote in the Greek Parliament. It was precisely the shortcomings and contradictions in this area that have so far allowed mistakes, delays and often images of chaos. Everyone knows what the new government has just received. Thousands of people stacked in the islands, scattered in the mainland, problematic structures - such as Moria - almost seventy thousand asylum applications remain pending and despite the huge waste that OLAF is investigating, only 22% of European funds have been fully exploited. One single element is enough to describe the situation that we have received and the policy of the previous government. Of the tens of thousands of immigrants and refugees who have arrived in our country over the last four and a half years, there have only been a thousand eight hundred six returns to Turkey. I repeat, there have only been a thousand eight hundred six refunds, when nearly seventy thousand asylum applications are currently pending. And the most returns that were made were voluntary returns, which proves in practice that the entire asylum system remained essentially in the form of prolonged paralysis, at the same time signalling that people may come to Greece to stay undetermined, without any control, but also giving Turkey the right not to apply the joint declaration signed with the European Union and also giving Europe the right to strongly criticise us, because we as a European country, a European border state, are not in line with our obligations under the joint declaration signed by the European Union with Turkey since 2016. Everyone also recognises that such a complex problem is more easily addressed at birth than five years after it has been gigantic and that the illusion of the open borders requires an organisation to adapt to the realistic policy of safe borders and that the idealization of immigrants who are'saving', takes boldness and time to become a daily life of a state and of citizens who care about it. But I'm not here to hide behind the weaknesses of others. Migration is already against us, and we have to deal with it both at European and national level. And we will address this despite the found shortcomings of even strong organisations, despite the different opinions and the different voices which are emitted today by the European Union itself, which has not yet reached a definitive policy on this issue, by complicating international solidarity, but also national responsibility and by looking at the hunters of the world and the rights of the Greeks. Ladies and gentlemen, as I said at the outset and as many Members of the House have developed, the great extent of the arrangements that we are discussing today is that they are establishing for the first time a solid international protection law, which serves three key principles: Firstly, it codifies five different laws, a presidential decree and dozens of scattered provisions or directives. All of this is in line with Community law and with the judgments of the European Courts. Secondly, it simplifies and shortens the granting of asylum to the real refugees, giving priority to children and families, to which it gives access immediately to health and education. And thirdly, it integrates those who seek protection in a context of respect and cooperation with the Greek authorities. Those who paradoxically claim asylum, but who will offend the country, do not have a place in our country. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] In short, it is an integrated response that gives the migration and refugee problem an organised, democratic state. For example, I will only mention a few forecasts that testify to this dual approach. Strong rules that put an end to illegality and arbitrariness but also sensitivity that gives additional rights to the wronged. From now on, each application should be examined within six months instead of nine which have been valid until now, and the decision should be given to the person concerned within ten days instead of twenty. If, in the meantime, the applicant does not cooperate with the authorities - he has left the country without permission - the request will automatically be considered unfounded and will of course be a candidate for departure. That is why the new system will first examine the cases of vulnerable persons and also those with infringemental behaviour, so that their different courses can be carried out in good time. The hospitality of whoever finds himself not applying for protection will also be interrupted immediately after his arrival, whether he has concealed property resources or has violated rules of operation of the structures in which he resides. The general 'traumatic stress' that you all know is difficult to ascertain by doctors and which - to say it quite simply - had become a familial, so that many could invoke it falsely, be regarded as vulnerable and transported in the inland. On the contrary, not only the unaccompanied and vulnerable people are now integrated. We are introducing new provisions in favour of real refugees. Members of the applicant’s family will no longer be considered, except for spouses and children, and their non-married partner. The same will apply to offmarried children and their parents and to those who generally have custody of a minor child. In particular, minors will be introduced at the respective primary and secondary levels. If their parents do not respect this obligation, they will have sanctions. Very soon we will be able to announce a special programme for the five thousand unaccompanied minors, of which unfortunately only a thousand today have the elementary care of an organised structure. After all, all legal refugees will have access to work. A special number will also give them access to care, and after six months they will also be part of special employment programmes to stop Greece from attracting foreigners, who have so far been seeking protection only to find work in another state. Ladies and gentlemen, it is clear that refugee migration flows are due to deep problems, to war crises, to major economic inequalities. Therefore, large-scale interventions are needed on the basis of international law, respect for the borders of the Treaties, peaceful cooperation between peoples and governments, financial support for countries that today have great difficulty in providing their citizens, their nationals, the prospect of a good life within their borders, and I have no problem repeating this now. But I'm also a realist. I see the difficulties, I recognize the medium-term data. I know very well that the drama of millions of fugitives is being used by some countries as a lever for its own geopolitical purposes, but also that of course there is also a field of action of circuits that trade very expensive and often in vain human hope. Apart from analyses, we must also have a specific action plan in this time and under the circumstances. At European level, it is well known that we are highlighting immigration as the new challenge for our entire continent, claiming common protection of its eastern borders. I therefore very much welcome the new mobility caused by the recent German initiative for a comprehensive review of European asylum policy. After all, I had discussed the Greek positions with the German Minister for the Interior, Mr Zehofer at the Maximos Concert Hall about a month ago. This issue, namely the need for Europe to expressly acquire a common asylum policy, a common policy on the issue of the refugee immigration issue, I put it at the recent summit, and urgently calling for a review of the Dublin system. That is where I put it - as I do in all my public interventions - and all the European countries before their responsibilities. I will repeat what I have also said to the Council that there cannot be countries which enjoy the benefits of the free movement of citizens that the Schengen Convention ensures and at the same time refuse to stubbornly even the minimum sharing of the refugee immigration problem, for reasons that they have to do with the internal balance of certain European governments alone. There can be no European solidarity. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] On the other hand, we are serving three needs. We protect our borders, save lives in the Aegean and land the islands of the eastern Aegean. Already in our seas, we are triggering a modern monitoring system. Port patrols are increased. We offer more thermal cameras and more flexible gears, floating means. Our target remains the same. No lost life in the Aegean and no inflammation of our waters without control. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] At the same time, in cooperation with the Ministry of Interior, the local government and the operational support of the Ministry of National Defence, we promote the organized relief of our islands by transporting refugees to the mainland. In this effort we want the co-operation of the municipalities and local communities. I have said many times that they should not under any circumstances be surprised. Instead, those who are willing to help in this great national effort must be rewarded. We ensure that this spread is carried out with caution in the number of refugees and with priority in families, so that this process can be made more acceptable to local communities. I repeat that they will be strengthened to have the power to assimilate this burden, this burden of emergency hospitality. So this policy is now being complemented by the Asylum Act. It is a law which, among other things, also sends a clear message: So far to those who know that they are not entitled to asylum, they are trying to get in and stay in our country. They will be returning either to Turkey quickly on the basis of the joint declaration or to their homeland, and will lose the money they gave to the disgusting circuits of the traffickers. At the same time, however, the new system also sets limits to the blurred action of certain non-governmental organisations, but also calls on me to say this - some who are consuming for sensitivity from some remote and theoretical observers, to enter the real battle on the ground and at last to take their responsibilities. Ladies and gentlemen, much of the world, unfortunately, lives today in conditions of economic tolerance and often of political freedom. Millions of people are potential applicants for international protection. The solution, however, is not to open the gates of the country wide, to welcome them all. This would condemn the Greeks themselves in denial of the dream but also condemn Greeks in a worse life, and this would in turn close our open-hearted and hospitable society, distort our traditions, ideals, eventually arm the fans of xenophobia and racism, as is unfortunately the case in many European countries. The policy of open borders is the same mistake as the policy of closed borders. We have lived ten years of crisis, yet we have lifted and lifted with dignity and fairness to our shoulders the responsibilities of three continents. Our country is well aware of the refugee situation, which is why it will give refuge to those in danger. But this will be done by rules, coordinates and structured. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] Only those who want it can and are entitled to healthy and educated tomorrow can be fertilely incorporated into the national trunk. Because I do not hide from you that as I admire the achievements of Giannis Antetokounbo, so I also delighted in the parade of 28 October the excellent student with origin from Africa, this girl who so proudly kept the blue-white. [[Applause from the New Democracy wing]] I'm closing up with one truth. Solidarity or it will be manifested by a system or simply there will be only in words and slogans. And that's not a point of view mine. It is the thought of a great hunter, philosopher Hannah Arent, who was persecuted as a Jew from Nazi Germany, fled refugee to France and from there in America, where he lived thirteen years without citizenship. It was her who defended as much the right to have rights as anyone used to say, but she was the same to say that human rights can only be guaranteed in practice by organised political communities. That is exactly what our current legislation defends. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-10-31-S1-commons.u353	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the government and the draft legislation are continuing on the path it has taken for four months. It continues to seek, as it has done for taxation, property and investment - as it does for immigration - to use common sense to respond to self-evident. And what's the self-evident here? It goes without saying that the international protection of foreigners in Greece must only be about the real beneficiaries. In other words, refugees cannot be economic immigrants. Refugees are not the criminals of ordinary criminal law. Refugees must not be those who refuse to cooperate with the authorities, give false evidence of origin, are infringing and so on. It was not only self-evident but also imperative that the legal structure that was created mainly in SYRIZA, based on the famous philosophy of justice, should be reviewed in different areas of our country. I would remind you of the liberation of foreigners who were found before 2015 in closed facilities with the new minister, Mr. Panoussis, who described the newly built Amydaleza as a concentration camp, the disappearance of foreigners who, after 2015, the next relevant Minister of SYRIZA Mr. Christodoulopoulos did not know where they ended up, but simply that they did not stay in hospitality centres because they preferred to be melted in the squares. And thirdly, the overaccumulation in the Aegean islands of all kinds of new foreigners who soon multiplyed when SYRIZA's theory of non-existence of maritime borders became known. Of course, the New Democracy never said that. Also, the non-discrimination between the different categories, as everyone was now considered vulnerable in one way or another. So it ended up in vulnerable groups more than 85% of immigrants and the most common reason was post-traumatic stress. [[At this point, the First Vice-President of Parliament, Mr Nikitas Kaklamanis, occupyes the President-in-Office of the Council.]] The question therefore arises: Why did SYRIZA, with all the special priorities it had set at the time - and which we all remember - rushed to open in 2015 an additional front? So, why did you almost immediately abolish the current illegal immigration management system created by New Democracy? Apart from your ideas, which undoubtedly played a role at that time, it seems that the political objective of the SYRIZA - ANEL Government was the permanent establishment in Greece of a large group of impoverished people who are willing to remain in the country dependent on the state, people who wanted to operate as a catalyst for the transformation of the public sphere, namely the transformation of both the state and the Greek society. And that's not what we say. It is said by the national strategy for the integration of immigrants from SYRIZA, published in June 2018. Unfortunately, not only were they written, but they were also attempting by a government which had no priority for the citizen but its own ideas. Ladies and gentlemen, the bill under discussion is an attempt to apportion all the distortions of the four-and-a-half years that have led to a clear vine in the area of immigration. This extends the right to detain foreigners at all stages of the process and for all persons. Non-compliance with transfer decisions will now lead to either a direct return or an absolute priority examination of their case in order to speed up their removal from the country. A general summary of the deadlines for examining requests to the first and second degrees shall be given. Failure to comply with the conditions of operation of the hosting structures will now lead to interruption of benefits and detention of foreign offenders. In addition, the issue of lists of foreigners with a shorter term of validity is being introduced. Their non-renewal for any reason will lead to a rejection of the substance of the application for international protection status. The vulnerability criteria are limited in order to identify from Greece the same criteria that other States of the European Union use. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member hits the warning bell.]] Mr President, I shall conclude. The second degree treatment of foreigners will no longer be completely suspensive and will not automatically give the country the right to remain. In each reservation, time will be measured from the outset and foreigners will therefore not be released because of the cumulative overrun of the eighteenth month. Finally, there is the restriction of access to work with six months' stay under conditions and a right to withdraw access to work in the event of a rejection decision. These, ladies and gentlemen, are some of the obvious contributions made by the new government to the asylum system. This is a common mind approach, which is trying to simplify and achieve the examination of requests for international protection. It is the obvious obligation on the part of the Greek state to operate the rule of law for all. Leaving away from ideologies and party purposes, we legislate self-evidently. I am sure that in this fixed path of flexicurity and development, our government and our homeland will continue. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2019-10-31-S1-commons.u460	Thank you, Mr President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, today's bill deals with a vital issue for our country. Greece, because of its geographical position, has been at the main gateway of refugees and migrants in Europe. To date, the management of this serious situation has been more than ineffective. The demonic legislative framework, the undersubmitment of asylum services and the lack of any serious planning they had brought our country into a borderline, leaving many people from third countries unprotected. At the end of last July, sixty-seven thousand asylum applications were pending and fourteen thousand appeals were awaiting examination, proving the scale of the problem. This is an extremely complex matter, and it is very much about the implementation of the European Union-Turkey Agreement. However, the national management of the matter is also important. Today's bill sets out a serious and understandable framework for dealing with the situation for the first time. First of all, what is being done for the first time with today's bill is to consolidate legislation on international protection, taking into account national and European case-law. To date, the relevant provisions have been dispersed and it has been extremely difficult for anyone to have a clear picture of what ultimately applies in any case and what is not. The organisation of individual provisions under this bill also agrees with the Citizens' Counsel, because this promotes legal certainty. The specific provisions of the draft law bring major improvements to all major issues. If applications for international protection are rejected, there will be a possibility of bringing an action, but now an application should be made by means of a document setting out specific reasons, otherwise it will be rejected as inadmissible. The integration of minors into primary and secondary education - so far I would remind you that it has not been compulsory - and in the event of a breach of this obligation, parents, adults of the family, as well as Greeks, are subject to the same sanctions. Also, in the new provision, applicants for international protection will not be able to enter the labour market immediately, but only if six months have elapsed since the application was lodged and there is no primary decision without the delay of the applicants themselves. A major and significant change is that there will now be a legitimate performance, even when the applicant itself cannot be identified, as is very often the case in practice. With the new provisions, performance will be legally performed to the attorney or authorised consultant. This will enable all deadlines to run and speed up return procedures. For the first time, it will be possible to decide to refund applicants even before a decision is taken on their application, as, for example, where the application has been rejected as manifestly unfounded. The creation of a list of safe third countries will significantly facilitate proceedings, since those from these countries will be presumed not to be entitled to protection unless specific conditions are invoked for themselves. Up to now 85% of immigrants were among vulnerable groups. The categories of those considered to be vulnerable are now identified and the post-traumatic stress is no longer the reason for vulnerability. A reasoned recommendation of the competent medical staff of the host centre is also required to refer them to social protection bodies. Finally, the incredible lawlessness that prevailed in hospitality is tackled decisively. With the new regulation, when we have a serious breach of the regulations on the operation of host centres and, above all, when violent behaviour occurs, there will be a break in material terms and a check on the possible execution of criminal offences. This will restore security within these areas. The implementation of the new provisions will speed up the procedures for examining requests for international protection. The return of those who are not entitled to protection will be made in a more direct way, since the rejection decisions will also be adopted. The measures that we are voting on today will help effectively to distinguish who really need protection, to return to their countries those who are not entitled to remain on European soil and ultimately to help those who need us on the basis of the principles of humanism, international law and the Geneva Convention. Thank you.

I accept that they have a higher civilization than we do. But it's not us and we don't want that. This is the real fear and the real corner of the Greeks today. That is why I think the draft law tabled today in this House, which is being voted, is in the right direction. That is enough - because, as Roydes said, in Greece, it is not enough for a law to be passed, it must also be implemented - that this law must be implemented properly and implemented in a timely manner so that those who really need international protection can obtain and stay in Greece and those who must not be able to leave and go abroad again where they come from. The first way is to codify many scattered regulations in a user-friendly legislative framework, in line with Community legislation, which will serve both administration and asylum seekers. Secondly, accelerating returns through the abolition of the previous appeal procedure and the delays that it entailed. Thirdly, the examination of each case is linked to respect for the rules and the rule of law by asylum seekers. Infringements will now have consequences. Fourthly, the functioning of the system is redesigned and reception and asylum services are strengthened. Fifthly, the mechanism for identifying and controlling asylum seekers is improved. And the humanitarian dimension has two main parameters: on the one hand, based on International Law and respect for the human dignity of entering our country, and on the other - and on the other - the protection of the smooth and peaceful livelihood of all Greek citizens. In order to address these problems that we have little - very much all known from our everyday lives, we need specific objectives and thus immediate implementation of them. And I explain: a clear legal framework, a correction of the incumbent until now to the Greek asylum system, a redesign of a new asylum system which is in line with the European Union's, in essence, respecting the rights of asylum seekers, not only by means of declarations, but also by acts and legislative actions. Here I would like to stress that this bill takes full account of all the facts currently prevailing in our country, the increased flows, the closed borders and, basically, the inability of Greece to welcome all third-country nationals who wish to enter and stay on the ground. We are protecting and laying down clear, concrete rules so that the administrative services, but also the applicants, are aware of exactly what is happening at legislative level. The speed from which this legislative framework is based makes it possible to manage rationally the applications from now on. It offers speed, it offers flexibility and it does not enclose asylum seekers in eternal and continuous procedures, which in fact benefited the illegal traffickers and those who, unfortunately, exploited these unfortunate people who arrived in our homeland. ParlaMint-GR_2019-10-31-S1-commons.u619	Mr. Kelles, from New Democracy, has the floor for five minutes.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-10-31-S1-commons.u620	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, with this draft law, the Government is putting us in order in the chaotic landscape of polynomialism, administrative uncertainty and idealism, inherited by the SYRIZA Government, which between 2016-2018 passed three relevant bills on international protection, and further amending provisions were added to two other bills. The result was a dissolved asylum system, for which anyone who dared to criticise, whether from local societies or from OTAs, was described as xenophobic or at best as extreme. That is the truth, ladies and gentlemen. It is also true that you left sixty-seven thousand asylum applications untreated. It is true that fourteen thousand appeals are pending. It is true that 60% of organic sites were empty. Which Ministry of Migration Policy are you talking about? There was nothing. And among the others, you have been circumventing in both the European Union and Turkey Agreement by resurrecting just one thousand eight hundred people in four years. And you baptized the immigration issue as a refugee. Since the day we took over the governance of the country, we have been taking steady recovery steps. We send messages in all directions and in pity that the country is not a clear vine. Everyone now realises that there is a serious government that protects borders and defends national interests. Apart from keeping borders, which is gradually intensified in Aegean and Evros, we are creating pre-regulatory centres for those who are not entitled to asylum. Our aim is for the returns and returns by the end of 2020 to reach 10,000. At the same time, and in accordance with our commitments, we are speeding up and tightening up asylum procedures. On the one hand, the examination period for applications is defined from six to nine months for the normal procedure and from twenty to thirty days for the rapidity, i.e. when they are vulnerable applicants, for those who pose a risk to national security or public order, and for those who are at the border or who have misleaded the authorities or who have not cooperated in fingerprinting. On the other hand, those who violate the terms and regulations of the centres will be faced with the cessation of housing, while those who do not comply with the decisions of their transfer to other structures, their application will be examined with absolute priority, taking into account the element of their non-compliance as an element capable of instigating their return process. At the same time, the appeal procedure also changes to the second degree and to the courts. First of all, the application form will not be given immediately but a justification form should be drawn up to justify the applicant's action. The Boards of Appeal will continue to function, but will be appointed by judges, while appeals will be brought to the administrative courts of Athens and Thessaloniki, which can respond rapidly to the appeals for annulment and where short deadlines may be set both for the judgments and judgments. Further, returns are accelerated. The decision to reject the application at first instance will allow the applicants to return if they do not bring an action. Because if the request is rejected, the applicant will be detained. However, it should be noted here that full protection of the rights and privacy of applicants will be taken. Three measures are also taken in areas which, until now, have favoured immigration. The first is finding a job. The applicant will be able to work if his application has not been answered within six months of his request. It is understandable that this will discourage immigration in conjunction with the second measure which is the training of lists of safe countries of origin and of safe third countries. Through the lists, the procedure for examining requests will be accelerated and refunds will be accelerated if the applicant who comes or has been passed through a safe country, indicates that his application is rejected or, respectively, that Greece is not responsible for examining his request. In this new context, the removal of post-traumatic stress as a reason for vulnerability is also added. Ladies and gentlemen, with this draft law, our Government unifys diffuse provisions and creates immigration policy, which had been abolished in the government of SYRIZA. At the same time, it goes on to separate the refugee from immigration. Asylum is sacred and must be given to those who really need it. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] I shall conclude, Mr President. Immigration is a phenomenon of geopolitical, economic and social. However, our country cannot lift its own burden, nor does it have the population and infrastructure to accommodate and assimilate all these people. By voting in favour of this draft law we ensure legality by adapting national legislation to modern challenges. At the same time, we continue to fight for the internationalisation of the issue. However, our European partners must assume their responsibilities, because we are not the government of willing men like our predecessors, but we are the government of all Greeks. Thank you.

I shall give the floor to the rapporteurs, if they so wish, so that they may be placed for three minutes each. The rapporteur of the New Democracy, Mr Cappatos, has the floor.
ParlaMint-GR_2019-10-31-S1-commons.u632	Thank you, Mr President. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I would like, in my speech, to point out, as a result of the pressure of time, three points which deserve to be raised, the detention, the operational capacity and our responsibility as a rule of law. As regards detention, the objections raised are excessive if we take into account the fact that a third-country national or a stateless person applying for international protection may exceptionally be held on the basis of an individual assessment and provided that alternative measures cannot be applied. Therefore, the concern expressed by many of our fellow Members does not exist in so far as it has been expressed, since we are talking about the exemption which is even individually assessed. In addition, if we consider the legislative improvement of Article 46 by adding the paragraph on the possibility for judicial authorities to decide on the legality of extending detention, I believe that the arguments put forward by colleagues in opposition are not founded. With regard to the readiness and management capacity of the administration, I would like to highlight two parameters. The first concerns the nature of the bill. The design of a codified, coherent and clear legislative framework cures precisely this problem, namely the ability of the administration to acquire a specific and fully understandable institutional environment that will enable it to improve its management capacity. In fact, the introduction and development of a new type of law, as the Minister himself pointed out, will provide the judicial officers and other services of the State with the institutional experience and education in the new reality. I now come to the last, which is the most important issue, the rule of law. We have heard, ladies and gentlemen, as well as any political disagreements, wordings offensive and provocative. In what sense can the designation as 'immoral' be justified for those who vote for the bill, Mr Varoufakis? On the other hand, how exactly are we going to help society solve its problems when we appeal to inferior instincts and resort to populist and politically inexpensive cries that undermine every embedded effort for social peace? Ladies and gentlemen, in a difficult management European crisis, the burden of first reception and identification fell on Greece. The massive entry of refugees - migrants found Greece unprepared and inertia against a problem that is constantly growing. Today, with this bill, we are voting on this picture to change. The timing is appropriate to draw up a new path, to rediscover those elements that characterize us as a nation and as a state, and we must now highlight the responsibility of solidarity on the one hand and a sense of security on the other. Today we are voting on Greece's inertia and procrastination. The international protection bill is our chance, as a European country, to work together, with a plan and effective response to a problem that we do not have the luxury of leaving for later. Thank you.
